# Weapons | Tactical Shooter Pack Unreal

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/weapons.md)
.

BP\_TacticalShooterWeapon[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/weapons#bp_tacticalshooterweapon)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This is the base weapon Blueprint class. It handles most player actions, including:

*   Draw/Holster
    
*   Firing
    
*   Inspecting
    
*   Mag Check
    
*   Reloading
    

### View Settings[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/weapons#view-settings)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FwR4wxvH8Urg84R7yayl3%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D99766b18-1854-4665-8974-9a659f097b6c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=cc1320f462468ccdb1ef82dccb787795&sv=3)

UE4 and UE5 settings.

Assign view settings for UE4 and UE5 mesh models here. When switching models, all weapons are forced to use the updated mannequin settings.

### Weapon Animations[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/weapons#weapon-animations)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252Fg9n2kicFkTEu1sHT6mhP%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D977564dd-b6c4-4cc4-9089-99252a4d3545&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=897f62751d7f9e79a37df9c2ec06f797&sv=3)

Weapon Anim Montages.

Assign all Anim Montages used by this weapon:

*   Draw
    
*   Holster
    
*   Reload Tactical
    
*   Reload Empty
    
*   Reload Start Empty
    
*   Reload Start Tactical
    
*   Reload Loop
    
*   Reload End
    
*   Inspect
    
*   Mag Check
    
*   Deploy Attachment (only used by Herrington 11-87 and SRM-12)
    
*   Stow Attachment (only used by Herrington 11-87 and SRM-12)
    
*   Fire
    
*   Fire Out
    

### Firing settings[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/weapons#firing-settings)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FOdPm5WNDHBTf4lH9xCe7%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6036cee7-0e62-4c21-9e17-899b6acff562&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=acb78e7d7da908557a8ab714922a2fbf&sv=3)

Recoil settings.

Here you can assign the [Recoil Animation Data](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/procedural-recoil#recoil-animation-data)
 and [Camera Shakes](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/first-person-camera#camera-shakes)
 assets.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FzPSIpqXwDnDDoBWOw7G1%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df533130c-14d0-45f8-9475-7655dd5934d1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=861f797ab2f6023e20fbdcbe92cb8a43&sv=3)

Weapon properties.

*   **Max Ammo**: Ammo capacity.
    
*   **Fire Rate**: Rounds per minute.
    
*   **Burst Length**: Number of rounds per burst.
    
*   **Use Bursts**: Enables burst mode.
    
*   **Use Full Auto**: Enables full‑auto.
    
*   **Is One Handed**: True for all pistols and **sidearms** (guns held with one hand).
    
*   **Active Fire Mode**: Default fire mode.
    

BPI Tactical Shooter Weapon[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/weapons#bpi-tactical-shooter-weapon)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This Blueprint Interface is used by Animation Blueprints to get essential weapon info.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FUon7UX1JqFvDdnbkJ6Od%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dfb96652a-418e-4ccf-8d4f-c4a9648dcbcb&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6c2314c301063b1eb40aecb7e864e4f6&sv=3)

Interface functions.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F5YGqvYWjdHogEyzjuXKU%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1f6efac2-3dc5-42dc-8f80-bfa3b6402e6b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c5fb2757e7a4ca93e3b7da23defb7722&sv=3)

Returns current active fire mode.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252Fxz0pkX7qOY2RQlA7HnX2%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D65f8fc16-fd88-4661-8d49-bf2cb62441b6&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=11827d62a60081e043f695ab112ada27&sv=3)

Returns aim point FTransform.

**Aim Point** is a socket transform relative to the weapon. In the example above, the default `AimPoint` socket is used; **Component** space means relative to the weapon.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FHqWw0jCJFmpdRnvGDwIN%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5afb6b25-9cd2-4fc6-afe8-812d53021993&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=894d46f095010d2232f4273f07403e18&sv=3)

Resetting the weapon.

**Reload Weapon** resets the current weapon ammo (e.g., when the reload animation ends).

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252Faz1Rai5Zb0jfSfvUKCki%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D73c5e983-d855-4f55-b8cc-6c59b84500c9&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9b9369a3c07b770973010b359fe1f5bc&sv=3)

Returns current and max ammo.

**Get Ammo** returns current and max ammo (used to animate magazine parts: spring, follower, cartridges).

Input blocking[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/weapons#input-blocking)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

While an action is playing (e.g., reload, mag check, inspect), weapons block other actions.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FjVFCwqmhJCLqMd76lac2%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db3f74f28-4910-41b7-8dd2-cc3cd5ff0b76&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=80d6d1fa405934d6fb14ab6b37b7f72b&sv=3)

Called when an action starts.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FV6vvKyFOapsat8rRVO94%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D14adbe66-71e2-40da-b8a9-07cdacd3652c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9eb0dbfdf160b88b4abcd86f8e1b9c88&sv=3)

Called when an action ends.

All actions have a check before starting an action:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FKhE7UYwebQ4hgAM4uKS7%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5626da2c-95f4-4887-a992-2a7d2b3dc65d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bdbd2f22889b76e00a01100dc5e7985f&sv=3)

Only play actions if no action is playing.

# Character | Tactical Shooter Pack Unreal

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character.md)
.

How it's animated[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#how-its-animated)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

All animations target the **UE5 Mannequin** skeleton and rely on Epic’s IK bone set:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FteFCaPYTuQupNUA6lYG0%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Db0272da6-699f-43ff-a7e8-ebb326dde088&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=db6bd5a5dc1a1db861a1fb7882d7b8c5&sv=3)

IK Bones

*   **ik\_hand\_gun** drives weapon movement..
    
*   **ik\_hand\_r** contains right‑hand animation data.
    
*   **ik\_hand\_l** contains left‑hand animation data.
    

The setup also uses **Virtual Bones (VBs)** for procedural animation:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FQRI7iIwfMZORammsXxmv%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D7c554bab-8649-4039-8b7e-441d44a3b1bf&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=b5e0e2741bb79aabeeaec061679e1044&sv=3)

Virtual Bone setup.

*   **VB ik\_hand\_gun** (targets **ik\_hand\_gun**): Used to position the pistol during quick draw so the **sidearm** is correctly placed relative to the **right hand**.
    
*   **VB ik\_hand\_gun\_pivot** (targets **ik\_hand\_gun**): All weapons are attached to this bone.
    
*   **VB hand\_r/l** (targets **hand\_r/l**): IK targets for the right/left hand.
    
*   **VB lowerarm\_r/l** (targets **lowerarm\_r/l**): Hint/pole targets for the right/left arm.
    
*   **VB recoil\_hand\_r** (targets **root**): Preserves right‑hand recoil when holding a pistol one‑handed.
    
*   **VB ik\_hand\_gun\_pose** (targets **root**): Used to compute the aiming pose.
    

**Tip**: Weapons are not parented to the right hand; they attach to **VB ik\_hand\_gun\_pivot**. This bone is blended in the Animation Blueprints so the final pose looks correct.

Animation Blueprints[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#animation-blueprints)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The pack uses modular **Animation Blueprints**:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FmcgIcrW0su4fxF2D7wYm%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da095252d-91a6-4968-af98-c24ded538cff&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9d426988f30369914219e22e477e8ea5&sv=3)

Animation Blueprints used in the pack.

### ABP\_TacticalShooter\_UE5[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#abp_tacticalshooter_ue5)

This is the main Animation Blueprint; it incorporates the other animation assets. Here’s how it works:

#### Base character pose[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#base-character-pose)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FXnrP6nGOxoaIhZOmhwRm%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D0014cd33-1f50-489b-ae00-30e7adb328a1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3d1d0e3790061cfc57fc210b92d814cf&sv=3)

Base pose.

The base combines a static idle **Anim Sequence** with the main **Anim Montage** (**DefaultSlot**).

#### Curve-based animations[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#curve-based-animations)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FOCVpGAIxteihqPng5HTU%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc076e6fc-2dcb-4248-8897-fad3c0b0a715&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1982a2e4f00b1bc5cedcbf258a656eca&sv=3)

Procedural movement graph.

General movement (idle, walk, sprint, jump) is curve‑driven and procedural, applied primarily via **Apply Additive** nodes.

The **Grounded** and **InAir** states provide animation curves such as:

*   **IK\_X**/**Y**/**Z**: position.
    
*   **IK\_Pitch**/**Yaw**/**Roll**: rotation.
    
*   **Camera Pitch**/**Yaw**/**Roll**: camera animation.
    

These curves are layered on top of the base pose. Finally, we blend **curve values** (not poses) using a helper bone:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FoSIRBVEn1M5hegrKwRDq%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D952ccfcd-afb1-4eec-b9f0-ba28002456e6&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=215011a257ec16810d2f7d6af1762302&sv=3)

Procedural movement blending.

**VB Curves** is a dummy bone used solely to blend curve values - not to animate a pose.

#### Pistol quick draw[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#pistol-quick-draw)

This layer applies quick‑draw and holster animations additively on top of the character pose:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FPLEbS1y6h7btJI1t63yt%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D265ace4c-42fc-4d27-81c4-28148ed5d1b1&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=06d55b4f7768b666cca78585ae866b63&sv=3)

Pistol Quick Draw layer.

Computes an additive pose per weapon, letting a single animation work across different poses.

**Tip**: Originally authored for **TR15**, but reused across weapons via this layer.Left hand actions

Apply left‑hand actions (e.g., take an item, push a door):

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252Fgbpgz18dV2feZcPyxbKp%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D05059ab1-f1b7-43a7-bde0-b30e28be7722&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3637f91a11db9902720eedc1a6a53086&sv=3)

Left Hand Actions layer.

#### Finger firing motion[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#finger-firing-motion)

Animate the right index finger while firing:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FfVytG91Su08JLFkOOVLg%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D93f910f7-e272-4230-8b3a-669bb2943449&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=59929c9a810519120606b81297896341&sv=3)

Finger firing motion.

A float smoothly interpolates to **1** while firing to rotate the finger, then returns to **0** afterward.

### ABP\_IK\_Pose[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#abp_ik_pose)

Positions the weapon and hands before the main procedural pass. It works in stages:

#### VB ik\_hand\_gun\_r[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#vb-ik_hand_gun_r)

First, override **VB ik\_hand\_gun\_r** with the base idle pose when quick‑draw is enabled:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FtveIWOu52DCtPdFysVmr%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D5906911d-8425-499e-855c-2a7fe7f6e08f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=079ef8579c8db8cb6bbf77bdb0c36e28&sv=3)

VB ik\_hand\_gun\_r override.

This is important for positioning the pistol properly.

#### Copying the animation data[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#copying-the-animation-data)

Copy the animation data from real bones to VBs. This lets us apply IK additively instead of **overriding** and breaking the pose:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F7NcgJMO1deugVmHvlCHX%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D323cf87a-49fe-4cdd-90aa-f5327eb29580&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3e308b24882516203910dcd267e39009&sv=3)

Copying the animation data.

Then override **VB hand\_r** and **VB hand\_l** with the current grip pose:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F4OPAgw3wK61k1X36qvM9%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9005c312-0e59-4ef4-aa85-11257100dda7&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6135d584f346dbbc36062d0eb91b90db&sv=3)

Left hand pose override.

By default, all weapons attach the left hand to the gun. You can smoothly disable this via a curve - for example, many animations **do this** to let the left hand move freely:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FbCEUaL8mUAfpvMYFlw0U%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df0e3322c-7cb9-475b-91ae-e1002dd7c9aa&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ece127df684cb3d191becf890112ea41&sv=3)

**Mask\_Grip** can disable the left‑hand attachment.

#### Offsets[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#offsets)

Finally, apply **FTransform** offsets to all IK bones.

### ABP\_IK\_Ads[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#abp_ik_ads)

Applies procedural aiming in two steps:

1.  Snap the weapon to the camera socket.
    
2.  Apply the **Aim Point** local transform.
    

Note: The **FPCamera** socket is attached to **neck\_02**:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F5lcTv1csf35Ao0EcnKez%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da8f2512c-8356-420b-8059-127042b5e1f2&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=adcd6995ebd6e37684f82814ecb51918&sv=3)

Camera socket.

### ABP\_IK\_Sway[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#abp_ik_sway)

Applies aiming and movement sway to **VB ik\_hand\_gun**. Sway is computed in **AC\_TacticalShooterAnimation**; this layer simply **retrieves** the **FTransform** and applies it to the bone:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FWIbsxkZaACOwbfc7R4hp%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8a4af3e6-814a-4e21-9531-c2ad0b999715&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ac7f4e097b9448616715de0596be174c&sv=3)

Sway layer.

### ABP\_IK\_Main[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#abp_ik_main)

The final Animation Blueprint, responsible for:

1.  Recoil
    
2.  IK Motions
    
3.  Leaning and Aim Offset
    
4.  IK
    
5.  Hiding the head bone
    

#### Recoil[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#recoil)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FmAzRV3NZfD6qz7crVBpl%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D302ecf71-fc85-4852-8c71-46f4349cced0&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6794eef1d6e609e108c04ffc6bd61ab6&sv=3)

How recoil is applied.

Recoil animation values are **retrieved** from the **Recoil Animation** component and applied via **Transform (Modify) Bone**. We preserve the right‑hand pose before applying recoil (needed for pistol quick draw).

#### IK Motions[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#ik-motions)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FemltrqMCE7gUd1SDWzoU%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da88ffa44-6b7c-46fe-86b0-86f1cdd33d57&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a64c7d802e9db96fc3b5d84a44463ef7&sv=3)

Additive motions

IK Motions are applied similarly to recoil. Motions include aiming and fire‑mode transitions ("boop"/"jiggle" animations).

#### Leaning[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#leaning)

Leaning distributes the angle evenly across spine bones:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252Fn3tZISYjGXhMOrOTW6ep%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De6cd8f26-cd3c-4267-8fb3-43e23df2dda5&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5e3f3ec4fe006edab816aac6b2421edc&sv=3)

Leaning rotation.

#### Curve-based animations[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#curve-based-animations-1)

Movement (idle, walk, sprint, jumping) is applied directly to **VB ik\_hand\_gun**:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FWEOaugYOs7UTZEbJ5yqp%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8edb89ed-b65d-419e-85dd-ffa0bd416db2&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=df2b380552de138abd632005c742ba96&sv=3)

Curve-based animations.

Curve values (**IK\_X/Y/Z**, **IK\_Pitch/Yaw/Roll**) are **retrieved** here and plugged into the respective translation and rotation axes.

#### Aim Offset[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#aim-offset)

Like leaning, **Pitch** is applied across spine bones to make the character look up and down:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F6AIv3qvYBu4adEoCKbkS%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D61d8e352-52c9-4637-a9b4-38be1d8ff901&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=20baac32647a39fdf1a4b2c238d68e2c&sv=3)

Spine bone rotation.

#### IK and head bone[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character#ik-and-head-bone)

Finally, **Two‑Bone IK** nodes apply IK using **VB hand\_r** and **VB hand\_l** as targets. We also scale the head bone to zero if `HideHeadMesh` is **true**:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252Ftw9KKtj5ac0UtK3IvAhn%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Ddf2b098b-40ec-493b-9e5f-3bec7c5af3ce&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3b9a53e6777fddd47a1e344e5670691f&sv=3)

Final steps.

# Weapons | Tactical Shooter Pack Unreal

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/weapons.md)
.

Weapon ABPs[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/weapons#weapon-abps)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

All guns have their own Animation Blueprint in **/Animations/UE5/**. Each has a simple structure consisting of a reference pose and a main **Montage** slot.

### WK-11 Viper ABP[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/weapons#wk-11-viper-abp)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FOXksF8xh2giO5WLakUKe%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D38f72a25-299d-439c-8f5c-fbc47a5fde0f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ad3a495b50f3c233ed51455cadb1db4d&sv=3)

ABP\_WK-11\_Viper.

*   **DefaultSlot:** Plays animations that animate the **bolt slide**.
    
*   **MagSlot:** Plays mag‑check and inspect animations to preserve the bolt‑slide state.
    

**Tip**: Bolt-slide state can change depending on the ammo state. For example, when out of ammo, the bolt will be locked back.

### TR15[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/weapons#tr15)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FbTkLP4JjW1uWHefF2Tj0%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbeece76d-3a28-4f9c-b8fd-b6cc46ed8884&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=d7242c5915f0261d4dca66cea6f99204&sv=3)

ABP\_TR15.

Similar to the WK‑11 Viper, **TR15** also preserves its bolt state.

*   **BoltSlot:** Plays all animations that animate the bolt.
    
*   **DefaultSlot:** Used for all other animations.
    

### Herrington 11-87[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/weapons#herrington-11-87)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FnyskUk3MapmiU6tzmn1w%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8ef895df-7474-4964-857d-872259606df8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c0deeabcc6bd36f0f5c45d1c253df3e3&sv=3)

ABP\_Herrington\_11-87.

*   **DefaultSlot:** Used for the inspect motion.
    
*   **DefaultReload:** Used for reload and firing motions.
    

### SRM-12[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/weapons#srm-12)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F01uMBtBSNdBRm4U9CHrg%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D16bf8ba1-40c5-4718-a8b8-fb876721dc2d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=9adfe1374ed539eeee2f260e659d693a&sv=3)

ABP\_SRM-12.

*   **DefaultSlot:** Used for animations that do not animate bipods.
    
*   **BipodSlot:** Used for bipod stow and deploy motions.

# Magazines | Tactical Shooter Pack Unreal

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/magazines.md)
.

Mag meshes[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/magazines#mag-meshes)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Magazine‑fed weapons (WK‑11 Viper, TR15, and SRM‑12) use separate Skeletal Meshes for magazines:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FMTfbsEcNnNtDTN3ZuSyi%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2a0e006c-35ad-48cb-b6f0-42463b12be78&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=cf5effad316b4e532f83eb76bbb5f408&sv=3)

TR15 base mesh.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FDttguHhyCQLJdRzpkBfK%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D85de6c30-691a-4170-a565-978b06d2c2e8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=12c37f8cf25e6c929cc7951e657e1752&sv=3)

TR15 magazine mesh.

Magazine skeletons each have their own Animation Blueprint, based on the **ABP\_Magazine** template.

ABPs[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/magazines#abps)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This Blueprint samples the current magazine ammo to drive animation:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252Fpm4HwKtpUy41NGmm029X%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De41f4a1e-3aba-4799-b80c-f664516b5c18&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a180abadf9b7ee8ccefd0c075c1926fe&sv=3)

How mags are animated.

Weapon‑specific Blueprints override only these essential properties:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F7aP8trvpc4rYGDcl4Ivs%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df0a692ed-ab87-4fac-be2e-310ec5fb2e84&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=77c7e09f3af29da7f8b6da73dbe4725c&sv=3)

Mag ABP properties.

*   **Magazine Animation**: Anim Sequence that animates the spring, follower, and cartridges.
    
*   **Mag Capacity**: Maximum ammo count for the magazine.
    
*   **Anim Time Step**: How much time (in seconds) it takes to process a single bullet.

# IK Motion Player | Tactical Shooter Pack Unreal

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/ik-motion-player.md)
.

Component[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/ik-motion-player#component)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**AC\_IKMotionPlayer** generates procedural shakes when aiming or changing fire mode. It exposes a single method:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FoiGucBmLG5pzlJkypyG0%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D2305c31d-9adf-4981-afec-805973a67505&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4cd04e427ec8649ff5dc4e1631275905&sv=3)

Use it to play a motion.

To extract the animation **values** (translation & rotation), access the `IKMotion` property:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FT8jD7sk7fmPpXD3xUPU4%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd5befd0a-7ce7-4412-b23f-3c6190f519bc&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=41e262e263df312507bf9753aeaf6fe8&sv=3)

IK additive animation.

IK Motion[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/ik-motion-player#ik-motion)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This Data Asset contains motion settings. To create a new IK Motion, right‑click and select **Misc → Data Asset → IK Motion**.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F1o5j1uYFSZ2aEj83kvzn%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8261fcd5-495a-486a-9f86-028d4d9b23ea&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c18b892043541a9446dae995cbbed5e6&sv=3)

IK Motion data asset.

*   **Position** and **Rotation**: Curves that define the animation for each translation and rotation axis.
    
*   **Position/Rotation Scale:** Controls the motion magnitude.
    
*   **Blend Time**: How long (in seconds) it takes to blend in a new motion.
    
*   **Play Rate:** Playback speed multiplier.
    

[PreviousTactical Shooter Animation](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation)

# First Person Camera | Tactical Shooter Pack Unreal

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/first-person-camera.md)
.

**AC\_FirstPersonCamera** is an Actor Component used to apply camera animations, recoil shakes, free look, and FOV updates at runtime.

Initialization[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/first-person-camera#initialization)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FXUwzJzqCjlRr6k24nnLP%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D0cc5ab13-1114-46fe-9d2a-37802fa5f94b&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=a29795352e8a69c581f3174513e4c026&sv=3)

Updating the target camera component.

First, specify that the **Camera** will be animated. Use the default `Camera` component that comes with `ACharacter`.

Then, update the player mesh reference:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FIXUXkKcOHuMCULTZIrqx%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D493c60f7-5c37-49d6-8c08-018950b44725&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6b80388c35e618b54aad048eaea7ffb0&sv=3)

Updating the character mesh.

Free Look[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/first-person-camera#free-look)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Free look is implemented by calling these methods on the Tactical Camera:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FiZGZ2EwAhZTgJ8wCmm37%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D70097474-fdef-496a-86ae-3e23680cb579&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=27210adc3254095d6ff7d7483cdfd58a&sv=3)

Handling free look.

And by controlling player look input:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FZIaEQZnqOxO2cGqfmh2T%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dec904c5e-e783-49c3-a3ae-40c8169a476c&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=187e65387655cf887900674932c50d68&sv=3)

Taking free look input into account.

**Note:** During free look, only the camera should receive look input.

FOV[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/first-person-camera#fov)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The camera also updates the FOV when aiming:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252Ff4MrR3r7f998AWfE39wb%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D28d89bf3-dd3e-4917-8f3a-6abd8c78b533&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=c53829e964eac227c63aa3cc80c1fc58&sv=3)

Updating FOV.

This logic switches between aiming and non‑aiming FOV states; the component handles FOV interpolation under the hood.

Camera Shakes[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/first-person-camera#camera-shakes)

-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The `Play Camera Shake` function is used to play shakes when firing:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FiKplA9gmN9os8sDo2NuX%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D4e26d630-7838-4ba3-b173-5c30264bbf8f&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=6ecf56f9bbb9e1e203e1b4695bc4800e&sv=3)

How to play a camera shake.

`DA_FirstPersonCameraShake` is a Data Asset that contains shake settings:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FleVxuCWiOIngpSoAre5t%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De9579923-f3f8-49d6-946f-d2c7b692fbed&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=006df1d3c6e676a98d94707fa5c4f072&sv=3)

Camera Shake settings.

*   **Rotation Curve**: Camera rotation animation.
    
*   **Pitch**/**Yaw**/**Roll**: Range of the pitch/yaw/roll value.
    
*   **Smoothing**: Interpolation speed.
    
*   **Play Rate**: Playback speed multiplier.

[NextTactical Shooter Animation](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation)

# Tactical Shooter Animation | Tactical Shooter Pack Unreal

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation.md)
.

**AC\_TacticalShooterAnimation** is a central Actor Component that serves as an interface between gameplay and animation. It stores and updates weapon settings and input values (e.g., leaning angle, current gait).

Tactical View Settings[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation#tactical-view-settings)

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This data asset is covered in detail here: [Procedural Animation Settings](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/procedural-animation-settings)
. To update the current settings, it's necessary to directly update the reference:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FKABA2KLdcq7OJGP1Pnzu%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D8255bc16-8435-4345-86ea-b2eb625ac4b4&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=bcfee56ac2cfa12160a35a4a53e549f4&sv=3)

Updating settings when equipping a new weapon.

Inputs[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation#inputs)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

This component also **stores** player input information, specifically:

### Leaning Input[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation#leaning-input)

Controls how much the player can rotate left and right to **peek** around corners:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FH4LWAQrZg3vQK2gBCeFM%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd364a831-0120-4577-b00c-8d2f4f58a5cc&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f3c5a024c53d55dd1fca42a36c592801&sv=3)

Updating leaning angle.

### Gait[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation#gait)

Represents the current movement state in the range **\[0–2\]**, where:

*   0 stands for idle
    
*   1 stands for walk
    
*   2 stands for sprint
    

Used to control the blending of idle, walk, and sprint animations:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FXTJHV0VY2qz7KhzjM97w%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D224b7211-7c80-473d-a963-fe54c2db9d92&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f5f8e18d2c3bbb0c890e7342feef33ae&sv=3)

Updating movement gait.

### Is Aiming[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation#is-aiming)

Used to control procedural aiming:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F0v9xgwBgjxabX5y1LjxM%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De0926fd4-f3ec-431b-a4a9-cd04ea349998&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f8ece169d0d012fd108ca5d4077c5999&sv=3)

Updating aiming.

### Is Firing[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/blueprints/tactical-shooter-animation#is-firing)

This flag has two applications in Animation Blueprints:

1.  Rotating the right index finger to press the trigger.
    
2.  Controlling the idle sway intensity when firing.
    

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F9pwSfMWEA0T2Y8faNhd1%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D19590f3a-0445-445a-810c-637ee319d018&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=533c8d0c229a77290b203f046c7c32c8&sv=3)

Whether firing is active or not.

# Procedural Animation Settings | Tactical Shooter Pack Unreal

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/procedural-animation-settings.md)
.

Tactical Shooter View Settings[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/procedural-animation-settings#tactical-shooter-view-settings)

---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Data Asset that contains animation properties used by gameplay and **Animation Blueprints**:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252F0fzlKXmwF3vWG7Qujdbq%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6c98ce8d-e0a5-4cdd-ae11-1d878f7723e3&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=f89779283059fa3f437830c72fa876c7&sv=3)

Tactical Shooter View settings.

### General[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/procedural-animation-settings#general)

*   **Idle Pose**: Base idle pose for the weapon.
    
*   **Character Animations**: Struct listing all **Anim Montages** used by the Blueprints.
    

### Procedural[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/procedural-animation-settings#procedural)

*   **Viewmodel**: Contains offsets for first‑person view.
    
    *   **Pivot Offset**: Weapon position offset.
        
    *   **Pose Offset**: FTransform offset for weapon and arms.
        
    *   **Sprint Offset**: FTransform offset, applied only when sprinting.
        
    *   **Right**/**Left Hand Offset**: Position offset for the right/left hand.
        
    *   LeftElbowSprintOffset: Left elbow offset applied only when sprinting.
        
    
*   **Pistol Quick Draw Viewmodel**: Has the same exact properties as the Viewmodel.
    
*   **Aiming**/**Movement Sway**
    
    *   **Stiffness**: Oscillation factor in range \[0;1\].
        
    *   **Damping**: Critical damping factor in range \[0;1\].
        
    *   **Interp Speed**: Smoothing speed.
        
    *   **Smoothing Speed**: How fast the value should go to zero.
        
    *   **Clamp Value**: Max allowed value.
        
    

Locomotion[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/procedural-animation-settings#locomotion)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

*   **Walk** and **Sprint Intensity**: Controls the influence of walk and sprint animations.
    

How these assets are used[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/procedural-animation-settings#how-these-assets-are-used)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3708064705-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252Fi3pgkdnEMLV7oBpvsWiN%252Fuploads%252FFK10ynKSsKHHbKKpURUO%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De7629298-ccb1-4a3e-8821-d470933f1676&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=e69ab5256d938f7c4b26d1a979f73942&sv=3)

All View Settings used in the package.

**Tactical Shooter View Settings** are used in both gameplay and **Animation Blueprints**. Each weapon has two settings assets: one for **UE5** animations and one for **UE4** animations.

**Tip**: The Tactical Shooter Pack allows switching **UE4** and **UE5** meshes at runtime.

[PreviousCharacter](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/character)
[NextWeapons](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/weapons)

## PRAS Documentation

- \[Welcome!\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/welcome.md): This is Procedural Recoil Animation System documentation.
- \[Recoil Animation\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics/recoil-animation.md): In this section we will cover the recoil animation details.
- \[Recoil Settings\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics/recoil-settings.md): In this section we will cover all recoil-related properties.
- \[Getting Set Up\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/tutorials/getting-set-up.md): In this tutorial you will learn how to set up the plugin.

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics/recoil-settings.md).

# Recoil Settings

All settings are located in the \*\*Recoil Data\*\* asset (to create one, right-click and go <mark style="background-color:purple;">\*\*Miscellaneous -> Data Asset -> Recoil Data\*\*</mark>). This asset contains many sections, and we will cover them one by one.

## Controller Recoil

<figure><img src="/files/H2GjXyCwZkbvO0rdrv4y" alt="" width="427"><figcaption><p>Controller Recoil.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Horizontal Recoil Step\*\*: a value will be computed in this range and added to the controller Yaw.
\* \*\*Vertical Recoil Step\*\*: a value will be computed in this range and added to the controller Pitch.
\* \*\*Recoil Smoothing\*\*: interpolation speed for X (Yaw) and Y (Pitch).
\* \*\*Damping\*\*: how fast the both Pitch and Yaw will go to zero,
\* \*\*Compensation\*\*: how much player input to take into account. 1 - 100%, 0 - 0%.

\*\*Controller Recoil\*\* is added directly to the \*\*Player Controller\*\* using \*AddPitchInput\* and \*AddYawInput\* methods.

## Curve

This section contains curves that we covered in the previous chapter:

<figure><img src="/files/c417f6g0rivuA65I6cOi" alt="" width="383"><figcaption><p>Vector curves for translation and rotation,</p></figcaption></figure>

## Input

These properties generate main target recoil values:

<figure><img src="/files/DQW7rwa90PAvMQtK7zl8" alt="" width="563"><figcaption><p>Input properties.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Pitch/Yaw/Roll Aim\*\*: a value will be generated in this range and added to the pitch/yaw/roll when aiming.
\* \*\*Pitch/Yaw/Roll\*\*: a value will be generated in this range and added to the pitch/yaw/roll when hip firing.
\* \*\*Kick/Kick R/ Kick Up Aim\*\*: a value will be generated in this range and added in the forward/right/up direction when aiming.
\* \*\*Kick/Kick R/ Kick Up\*\*: a value will be generated in this range and added in the forward/right/up direction when aiming.

It's worth noting how the random generation happens in runtime:

\* For 2D vectors, it will compute a value via FRandRange(X, Y)
\* For 4D vectors, it will compute a value via FRandRange(FRandRange(X, Y), FRandRange(Z, W)). We do it this way to avoid getting close-to-zero values, which sometimes look horrible.

## Smoothing

This group is only applied when auto/burst firing.

<figure><img src="/files/rbLfA5EiG3V9c1ZuAVG1" alt="" width="476"><figcaption><p>Smoothing properties.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Smooth Rot/Loc\*\*: interpolation speed for a rotation/translation axis. If the value is zero - no interpolation will be applied.
\* \*\*Multi Rot/Loc\*\*: multipliers for a rotation/translation axis.
\* \*\*Smooth Roll\*\*: if roll sign should change every shot. Keep it true to achieve the best results.

## Noise Layer

This layer applies translation 2D noise in the YZ plane (right and up movement).

<figure><img src="/files/UPI61Dsl8ZhCe0rEve3Z" alt="" width="416"><figcaption><p>Noise properties.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Noise 2DTarget\*\*: this value will be added to the translation of every shot.
\* \*\*Noise 2DAccel\*\*: interpolation speed for right and up axes.
\* \*\*Noise 2DZero\*\*: how fast each axis will go to zero.
\* \*\*Noise Aim Scalar\*\*: multiplier applied when aiming.

## Pushback

This layer is applied on the second shot of a burst or auto sequence.

<figure><img src="/files/3cetH5ktj2SsVKWc89H9" alt="" width="305"><figcaption><p>Pushback properties.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Pushback\*\*: this value will be added to the forward translation.
\* \*\*Pushback Zero\*\*: how fast the pushback should go to zero.
\* \*\*Pushback Accel\*\*: pusback interpolation speed.

## Recoil Sway

This layer applies sway to simulate a body recoil.

<figure><img src="/files/mhNVjnwao1iOUyyTgkMM" alt="" width="533"><figcaption><p>Recoil Sway properties.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Pitch Sway\*\*: a value will be randomly generated in this range and added to the pitch.
\* \*\*Yaw Sway\*\*: a value will be randomly generated in this range and added to the yaw.
\* \*\*Roll Sway Multiplier\*\*: will multiply the yaw result by this value and add it to the roll.
\* \*\*Damping\*\*: how fast the sway should go to zero.
\* \*\*Acceleration\*\*: interpolation sway speed.
\* \*\*Ads Scale\*\*: this value will scale the recoil sway when aiming.
\* \*\*Pivot Offset\*\*: will offset the recoil physical pivot.

## Timeline

These properties control the general animation timeline.

<figure><img src="/files/Sc8ehwvvcxTV55Pcdyep" alt="" width="291"><figcaption><p>Tineline properties.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Playback Offset\*\*: will offset the loop time interval. For example, if a weapon fire rate is 600RPM, the loop interval will be 0.1s + Playback Offset.
\* \*\*Play Rate\*\*: animation speed multiplier.

## Pivot

This section controls the physical recoil pivot point.

<figure><img src="/files/CCqfbhXdZt0EeUliCuqP" alt="" width="522"><figcaption><p>Pivot settings.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Pivot Offset\*\*: applied when hip firing.
\* \*\*Ads Pivot Offset\*\*: applied when aiming.

{% hint style="success" %}
\*\*Tip\*\*: pivot offsets can be useful when we want to apply recoil rotation around a certain point, like a shoulder.
{% endhint %}

---

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics/recoil-animation.md).

# Recoil Animation

## How Does It Work?

The \*\*PRAS (Procedural Recoil Animation System)\*\* uses an advanced, yet simple way to generate realistic recoil. The system takes Vector Curves for translation and rotation, plays them in runtime, and uses curve values as interpolation alphas to reach the randomly generated targets.

> RecoilAnimation = Lerp(0f, TargetValue, CurveValue)

where \*\*Target Value\*\* is randomly generated when a shot is fired.

<figure><img src="/files/8jf1BGmrDCIGcu2fShoB" alt="" width="563"><figcaption><p>Example recoil curve.</p></figcaption></figure>

Recoil curves should be in the range \\\[-1;1\], so a curve value can be used as an interpolation alpha. The curve peaks at 1 and then goes down to 0 with some oscillation.

{% hint style="success" %}
\*\*Tip\*\*: because curves are much simpler in comparison to other methods (e.g. spring interpolation), the recoil animation is frame-rate independent.
{% endhint %}

## Single and Auto Fire

\*\*Recoil Data\*\* is an asset, which contains all information about the recoil animation. This includes curves and other settings:

<figure><img src="/files/c417f6g0rivuA65I6cOi" alt="" width="383"><figcaption><p>Curve section in the Recoil Data.</p></figcaption></figure>

To create a new \*\*Recoil Data\*\*, right-click and go to <mark style="background-color:purple;">\*\*Miscellaneous -> Data Asset -> Recoil Data\*\*</mark>. This asset includes 4 \*Vector Curves\* in total: 2 for translation and 2 for rotation. The PRAS has 2 main fire modes:

\* Single
\* Auto/Burst (there's no technical difference animation-wise)

{% hint style="success" %}
\*\*Tip\*\*: we use different curves for single and auto fire because of animation quality. If we use single-fire curves when auto-firing, the animation will look choppy. To make it look smoother and more fluid we use a different set of curves.
{% endhint %}

Let's compare an example single and auto fire curve:

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Single" %}

<figure><img src="/files/C4eGmhULwBcP9XoAOYEU" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Auto/Burst" %}

<figure><img src="/files/JarShzoRAKsAxFnmFxCK" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

The curves are almost identical! The only difference is that auto curve values are very close to 0 at some time - this time equals the fire rate of a weapon (in this case 600RPM = 0.1s). This will ensure that the weapon's main muzzle climbing will be fully preserved when auto-firing.

{% hint style="danger" %}
\*\*Tip\*\*: regardless of a fire mode, the first shot is \*\*always\*\* a single fire shot.
{% endhint %}

---

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/tutorials/getting-set-up.md).

# Getting Set Up

{% hint style="success" %}
\*\*Tip\*\*: make sure to already implement basic firing logic in your project.
{% endhint %}

## Step 1 - Recoil Animation Component

First, it is essential to add the \*\*Recoil Animation Component\*\* to the character.

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Blueprints" %}

<figure><img src="/files/9AIoxInjzFLHHyFOvwCJ" alt="" width="419"><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="C++" %}

\`\`\`cpp
UCLASS(config=Game)
class AYourCharacterClass : public ACharacter
{
    GENERATED\_BODY()

public:
    AYourCharacterClass();

protected:
    UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, BlueprintReadOnly, Category = "Recoil")
    TObjectPtr<URecoilAnimationComponent> RecoilComponent;

    ...
};
\`\`\`

{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

Then, in your character class click on the RecoilAnimation component and specify the Helper UI:

<figure><img src="/files/E55tULMMrnHuCy6F57am" alt="" width="384"><figcaption><p>Select this blueprint.</p></figcaption></figure>

The Helper UI is useful when you want to modify the recoil values in runtime in a very convenient way:

<figure><img src="/files/vsDM6TkOjidcz8O2hYW0" alt="" width="533"><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

To enable this Helper UI in the game you can use the Blueprint-exposed methods:

<figure><img src="/files/UOmn1tme40ZXVfp4uOK1" alt="" width="485"><figcaption><p>How to use helper UI.</p></figcaption></figure>

## Step 2 - Initialize and Play

Make sure to call the \`Init\` method of the \*\*Recoil Animation Component\*\* to initialize the recoil animation:

<figure><img src="/files/vIutw7LqZIOZMyyOcj5D" alt="" width="563"><figcaption><p>Init function.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Data\*\*: \*\*Recoil Data\*\* asset.
\* \*\*Rate\*\*: fire rate or RPM (Rounds-per-minute).
\* \*\*Burst:\*\* length of the burst sequenc&#x65;\*\*.\*\*

Next, call \`Play\` and \`Stop\` methods when firing:

<figure><img src="/files/kZHSPwN8T08gsi947yR7" alt="" width="491"><figcaption><p>Firing logic.</p></figcaption></figure>

{% hint style="danger" %}
\*\*Note\*\*: Play must be called every shot!
{% endhint %}

## Step 3 - Animation Blueprint Integration

Make sure to add a \*\*Recoil Animation\*\* node to the Anim Graph:

<figure><img src="/files/0RCpZe9giFwQbHF8hpRq" alt="" width="234"><figcaption><p>This node will apply recoil.</p></figcaption></figure>

If you already have an \*IK\* system set up, select the \*IK\* or a \*VB\* (virtual bone) you want to animate with recoil in the node settings:

<figure><img src="/files/31LwKHeuOIuxweSwK8Fy" alt="" width="431"><figcaption><p>Node settings.</p></figcaption></figure>

### If you need help with IK

First, add Virtual Bones to the character head:

<figure><img src="/files/bkHmAf1ZP27VEKi5dyL7" alt="" width="305"><figcaption><p>VB stands for Virtual Bone.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*VB WeaponPivot\*\* targets ik\\\_hand\\\_gun.
\* \*\*VB hand\\\_r\*\* targets hand\\\_r.
\* \*\*VB hand\\\_l\*\* targets hand\\\_l.

Add \*\*CopyBone\*\* nodes before the \*\*Recoil Animation\*\* nodes:

<figure><img src="/files/odGQisKeqv5wld0vX4Jv" alt="" width="563"><figcaption><p>These will copy animation from FK bones to VBs.</p></figcaption></figure>

If your animations do not keyframe the \*\*ik\\\_hand\\\_gun\*\* bone (it is used for baking gun movement into the character animation), you will have to use \*\*hand\\\_r\*\* as a source bone in the first \*\*CopyBone\*\* node. You can additionally offset the \*\*VB WeaponPivot\*\* with a custom offset if you want more flexibility.

Finally, use \*\*Two Bone IK\*\* after the \*\*Recoil Animation\*\* node:

<figure><img src="/files/RfpWKgryHuoLDu8EMS8M" alt="" width="524"><figcaption><p>IK pass.</p></figcaption></figure>

<figure><img src="/files/8El7zczzOu77kCyONVY8" alt="" width="430"><figcaption><p>Preferred IK settings.</p></figcaption></figure>

\*\*\*

At this point, the setup is complete. If recoil is still not playing, double-check the steps above or:

\* Check if \*\*Recoil Data\*\* has any values.
\* The correct bone is animated by the \*\*RecoilAnimation\*\*.
\* \*\*Play\*\* method is called every shot.
\* The \*\*Fire Rate\*\* is in rounds-per-minute and greater than zero.

If the problem persists, feel free to post a ticket on our \[Discord\](https://discord.gg/kinemation-1027338787958816860).

# How to implement attachments

## Overview

To modify the current recoil animation, \*\*PRAS\*\* has the Input Scale feature using these methods:

<figure><img src="/files/eS7Eby7Ci9lvLS2rOBQ8" alt="" width="437"><figcaption><p>Input Scale methods.</p></figcaption></figure>

\* \*\*Scale Input\*\*: applies an Input Scale.
\* \*\*Unscale Input\*\*: reverts an Input Scale.
\* \*\*Reset Input Scale\*\*: resets any applied modifiers.

\*\*Input Scale\*\* is a struct that includes these properties:

<figure><img src="/files/7Bz73YY6k0M5uRGWkl6q" alt="" width="348"><figcaption><p>Input Scale properties.</p></figcaption></figure>

## Use-case

When attachment is equipped, make sure to update the current Input Scale:

<figure><img src="/files/31zDWxxgMmMrTdH4kndq" alt="" width="422"><figcaption><p>How to scale recoil.</p></figcaption></figure>

To revert changes of the previous attachment:

<figure><img src="/files/xUx87sEhPICGavu31ntq" alt="" width="423"><figcaption><p>How to revert recoil scale.</p></figcaption></figure>

# Getting Set Up | PRAS Documentation

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/tutorials/getting-set-up.md)
.

**Tip**: make sure to already implement basic firing logic in your project.

Step 1 - Recoil Animation Component[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/tutorials#step-1-recoil-animation-component)

------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

First, it is essential to add the **Recoil Animation Component** to the character.

Blueprints

C++

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252F4wyQRZRa2i08pclSWsQj%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da18eb5bc-9d03-4725-a1d1-6ed14f92dc5a&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=4c6f80366007eddd0252fe93d4a1d055&sv=3)

Copy

    UCLASS(config=Game)
    class AYourCharacterClass : public ACharacter
    {
        GENERATED_BODY()
    
    public:
        AYourCharacterClass();
    
    protected:
        UPROPERTY(EditDefaultsOnly, BlueprintReadOnly, Category = "Recoil")
        TObjectPtr<URecoilAnimationComponent> RecoilComponent;
    
        ...
    };

Then, in your character class click on the RecoilAnimation component and specify the Helper UI:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252F9Gmeeb2aeyrSNgY2rKZT%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D19855adf-c124-4b72-8197-61d089727171&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=35847383bc7ded05047e46d6e5c366ca&sv=3)

Select this blueprint.

The Helper UI is useful when you want to modify the recoil values in runtime in a very convenient way:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FLRJ92IQm7scBIPm2Ggci%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dca8d9f41-ad58-467a-aaf1-dfe2acf7f97e&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3f520ba02da9f6d3a17fd3470885ac03&sv=3)

To enable this Helper UI in the game you can use the Blueprint-exposed methods:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FSKhTkMdSkG87OHj1IOBh%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Df9e799a4-7c55-4a5c-a947-b6fe0c53a845&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3b549b165ade8ecb3c9c903fdc9cef41&sv=3)

How to use helper UI.

Step 2 - Initialize and Play[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/tutorials#step-2-initialize-and-play)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Make sure to call the `Init` method of the **Recoil Animation Component** to initialize the recoil animation:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FNGo3aI0bJbI5aiaxiC3O%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D57ca488f-80ac-4be5-84fc-38fe8c8a2d88&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=7c9e8c45dbe71d7b1ca373e4fe24c03b&sv=3)

Init function.

*   **Data**: **Recoil Data** asset.
    
*   **Rate**: fire rate or RPM (Rounds-per-minute).
    
*   **Burst:** length of the burst sequence**.**
    

Next, call `Play` and `Stop` methods when firing:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252Fwwsbla8WWkwDMOerwant%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dde35e9d4-4b22-43d3-ad21-aecf5faefa61&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=528ede1aa2700312b8c58cebbfedac6d&sv=3)

Firing logic.

**Note**: Play must be called every shot!

Step 3 - Animation Blueprint Integration[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/tutorials#step-3-animation-blueprint-integration)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Make sure to add a **Recoil Animation** node to the Anim Graph:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252Ft4NrSaox5umi2XKQr6uv%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D9d748092-77ba-4b30-a325-dc644d87c46d&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=dc3cda920b342d0b9850c1590ea77138&sv=3)

This node will apply recoil.

If you already have an _IK_ system set up, select the _IK_ or a _VB_ (virtual bone) you want to animate with recoil in the node settings:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FCWI5cErNp3NRPB4rNvaa%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dbdaa648d-13e4-4032-a727-4baf75290c20&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=944728471fc5a5a5334d4ba2c34188ea&sv=3)

Node settings.

### If you need help with IK[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/tutorials#if-you-need-help-with-ik)

First, add Virtual Bones to the character head:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FKir2cdAMDEQdfBhHoMeY%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D3d830ad4-5599-4786-910b-1e2e8f1f1389&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=2c4935b0dfc6000a69777917c0d2cd46&sv=3)

VB stands for Virtual Bone.

*   **VB WeaponPivot** targets ik\_hand\_gun.
    
*   **VB hand\_r** targets hand\_r.
    
*   **VB hand\_l** targets hand\_l.
    

Add **CopyBone** nodes before the **Recoil Animation** nodes:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252Fw1UK9d1se7me6b0kCKdH%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dd184c38a-565f-4f8c-8c3c-92011158cdea&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=08dcaa63f03d4a84919dfa322b6c7763&sv=3)

These will copy animation from FK bones to VBs.

If your animations do not keyframe the **ik\_hand\_gun** bone (it is used for baking gun movement into the character animation), you will have to use **hand\_r** as a source bone in the first **CopyBone** node. You can additionally offset the **VB WeaponPivot** with a custom offset if you want more flexibility.

Finally, use **Two Bone IK** after the **Recoil Animation** node:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FAMxlxBIIVVW97C4GAtzV%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3De38e011e-3271-4b84-a47b-f1e37908cdf9&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=5e776a4322b16d97b3d5ae38cf8f618c&sv=3)

IK pass.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FGo9YyPxponov5NI7JjCo%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Ddc7bee46-946a-4cc7-abe9-72bbf8c12fd7&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=1b3fb508d31f44445334d0c204c88124&sv=3)

Preferred IK settings.

* * *

At this point, the setup is complete. If recoil is still not playing, double-check the steps above or:

*   Check if **Recoil Data** has any values.
    
*   The correct bone is animated by the **RecoilAnimation**.
    
*   **Play** method is called every shot.
    
*   The **Fire Rate** is in rounds-per-minute and greater than zero.
    

If the problem persists, feel free to post a ticket on our [Discord](https://discord.gg/kinemation-1027338787958816860)
.

[PreviousRecoil Settings](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics/recoil-settings)
[NextHow to implement attachments](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/tutorials/how-to-implement-attachments)

# Recoil Animation | PRAS Documentation

For the complete documentation index, see [llms.txt](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/llms.txt)
. This page is also available as [Markdown](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics/recoil-animation.md)
.

How Does It Work?[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics#how-does-it-work)

----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The **PRAS (Procedural Recoil Animation System)** uses an advanced, yet simple way to generate realistic recoil. The system takes Vector Curves for translation and rotation, plays them in runtime, and uses curve values as interpolation alphas to reach the randomly generated targets.

> RecoilAnimation = Lerp(0f, TargetValue, CurveValue)

where **Target Value** is randomly generated when a shot is fired.

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FbGyBFT8oA2snyTlu8Z2W%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dc597fb21-26e5-4a41-a303-be413e5b20f8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=ab012f8512cbba09cb6e79c6e08eded9&sv=3)

Example recoil curve.

Recoil curves should be in the range \[-1;1\], so a curve value can be used as an interpolation alpha. The curve peaks at 1 and then goes down to 0 with some oscillation.

**Tip**: because curves are much simpler in comparison to other methods (e.g. spring interpolation), the recoil animation is frame-rate independent.

Single and Auto Fire[](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics#single-and-auto-fire)

-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------

**Recoil Data** is an asset, which contains all information about the recoil animation. This includes curves and other settings:

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252Fuf3hCnpZyMVd7Gru8iuJ%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Da0bf6c2d-e352-43b1-a343-f447802bd471&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=620c9871d93f733f5c8cad9862e003c3&sv=3)

Curve section in the Recoil Data.

To create a new **Recoil Data**, right-click and go to **Miscellaneous -> Data Asset -> Recoil Data**. This asset includes 4 _Vector Curves_ in total: 2 for translation and 2 for rotation. The PRAS has 2 main fire modes:

*   Single
    
*   Auto/Burst (there's no technical difference animation-wise)
    

**Tip**: we use different curves for single and auto fire because of animation quality. If we use single-fire curves when auto-firing, the animation will look choppy. To make it look smoother and more fluid we use a different set of curves.

Let's compare an example single and auto fire curve:

Single

Auto/Burst

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252Fj9OuQEgdKvPOsUFSmIo0%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1956567a-ad4c-4338-9664-9a3ea89beab8&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=3f5992d7868c2638b97e73e9f6f9d0e2&sv=3)

![](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3651122009-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FmWlhCRI70uShLRiOey3T%252Fuploads%252FS656FcC1900ZLIaUsT2X%252Fimage.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D949f1a80-ef7b-4791-a2e3-5cbccb400760&width=768&dpr=3&quality=100&sign=0dce328e36970fda7525342a21b5e7ee&sv=3)

The curves are almost identical! The only difference is that auto curve values are very close to 0 at some time - this time equals the fire rate of a weapon (in this case 600RPM = 0.1s). This will ensure that the weapon's main muzzle climbing will be fully preserved when auto-firing.

**Tip**: regardless of a fire mode, the first shot is **always** a single fire shot.

[NextRecoil Settings](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/pras-documentation/basics/recoil-settings)

# Unknown

\> For the complete documentation index, see \[llms.txt\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/llms.txt). Markdown versions of documentation pages are available by appending \`.md\` to page URLs; this page is available as \[Markdown\](https://kinemation.gitbook.io/tactical-shooter-pack-unreal/animations/camera.md).

# Camera

## How it works

The camera is animated using three curves:

\* \*\*Camera\\\_Pitch\*\*
\* \*\*Camera\\\_Yaw\*\*
\* \*\*Camera\\\_Roll\*\*

<figure><img src="/files/tWSkLsWtsc0Axur24qot" alt="" width="563"><figcaption><p>Camera animation curves.</p></figcaption></figure>

These curves are on the character skeleton, so whenever a Montage plays at runtime, it animates the character and camera simultaneously.

## How curves are created

You can find camera animations in each weapon’s \*\*Camera\*\* folder. These sequences aren’t used directly; they are processed by the extraction tool, which transfers camera curves to the character animation:

<figure><img src="/files/a7Y08sYloMC0L5W3CmND" alt="" width="359"><figcaption><p>Camera Motion Extractor.</p></figcaption></figure>

{% hint style="success" %}
\*\*Tip\*\*: Use this modifier to re‑extract or adjust the intensity of camera animations.First Person Camera
{% endhint %}

Actor Component applies camera animations by sampling curve values at runtime:

<figure><img src="/files/JKNhhGodFGngyjFZZsqJ" alt="" width="465"><figcaption><p>Camera animation.</p></figcaption></figure>

{% hint style="success" %}
\*\*Tip\*\*: This component also plays camera recoil shakes.
{% endhint %}
